# Deferred list — Dioreo (`db-deferred-list.md`)

**Dioreo's own deferred work**: confirmed bugs, time/condition reminders, the maintenance / tech-debt long-tail, and the handful of features big enough to warrant their own dedicated session. If a session working *only* in this repo would need to know it, it belongs here.

**History:** split out of the cross-project tracker on **2026-07-25 15:56 EDT** (tracked in-repo, so it gets real `git diff`/`git log` history like the rest of `docs/`), then **renamed and completed 2026-07-25 21:43 EDT** — that first pass moved only the tech-debt list and left this project's bugs, reminders, and resolved items sitting in the cross-project file, which defeated the point. This pass pulled all of them in, added the priority legend, and moved resolved entries out to `docs/archive/resolved-list.md`. `deferred-items.md` → `db-deferred-list.md`; the cross-project file is now `/Applications/Claude Code/meta-deferred-list.md`.

## Closing the loop back to the notes file
When a Queued/bug item here that was **filed FROM `docs/ideas/diors-notes.md`** ships or gets fixed, go back and check off (or reply to) the original bullet in the SAME session — don't let it wait for a separate sweep. Added 2026-08-03 19:36 EDT after the calendar-banner feature (filed here 2026-07-31, shipped as v2.46.0 the same day) sat unmarked in the notes file for 3 days and multiple sessions, because closing THIS file's entry was treated as the finish line and the originating bullet was never revisited — even though the shipping commit's own code comment cited the notes-file line number. A filed item's real origin is worth a quick grep for ("notes L###" or the item's own wording) whenever you check something off here.

## What is NOT in this file
- **The feature roadmap** — `docs/ROADMAP.md` is authoritative (v2 remaining · v3 · v4 · v5 · housekeeping). This file is deliberately NOT a copy of it: pointer, not duplicate.
  **⇄ Reality check 2026-07-27 19:45 EDT:** 7 items were found in BOTH files, so "not a copy" was not holding on its own. Each now carries a `⇄` cross-reference to its twin. Division of labour: **ROADMAP = which version it belongs to; this file = size, decided design, and when it gets a session.** Appearing in both is allowed — appearing in both *without* the `⇄` marker is the bug.
- **Resolved / dropped items** — `docs/archive/resolved-list.md` (except standing 🚫 decided-no calls, which stay below on purpose so nobody re-raises them).
- **Cross-project work, MarkEdit-extension bugs, Claude/Anthropic product feedback** — `/Applications/Claude Code/meta-deferred-list.md`.
- **Design gaps we're knowingly living with** — `docs/reference/platform-constraints.md`.

## 🏷️ Priority & effort tags
Every OPEN item carries `[Priority · Effort]` (+ optional flags). Two axes: one for *what to focus on*, one for *what can be bundled cheaply*. Resolved items don't need one.
- **Priority** (urgency + impact + risk + blocking): **P0** now (broken+user-facing / blocking / due) · **P1** soon (clear value, no reason to wait) · **P2** eventually (real, not pressing) · **P3** someday/parked.
- **Effort** (scope ONLY — ⚠️ it does NOT carry the model choice; see the grid below): **XS** minutes · **S** part of a session · **M** a session · **L** its own big/multi-session job. ⚠️ **No `XL` tier, deliberately.** Measured 2026-08-06: completion is **S 35% · M 28% · XS 14% · L 11%** (1 of 9, and that one was retired). L is already a parking label, so a bigger bucket would make parking easier. ✅ **Rule instead: an `L` item carries a named FIRST SLICE (an S or M) or a decomposition, or it is not schedulable.**
- **Model + reasoning effort** — a **separate axis**, chosen from task properties, never from the effort tier. Rows = *premise risk* (are the facts given and checkable?), columns = *deliberation load* (one place → whole system):

  | | Delib Low | Med | High | Very high |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | **Premise Low** | `Sonnet5-Low` | `Sonnet5-Medium` | `Sonnet5-High` | `Sonnet5-XHigh` |
  | **Premise Med** | `Sonnet5-Medium` | `Sonnet5-High` | `Sonnet5-XHigh` | `Opus5-High` |
  | **Premise High** | `Opus5-Medium` | `Opus5-High` | `Opus5-XHigh` | `Opus5-Max` |

  Effort buys **breadth**; model buys **judgement/self-correction**. Every cell is exactly ONE combo — if torn, take the lower and say why. Off-grid only by naming a trigger: `Sonnet5-Max` (exhaustive sweep, costly miss — usually beaten by writing a script) · `Opus5-Low` (downshift for high-volume triage). Escalate on EVENTS: two wrong hypotheses → +1 effort · a false premise → Sonnet→Opus · silent-failure surface → Sonnet→Opus · 🔻 **scope turned out mechanical → step DOWN.** ⚠️ **Never calibrate on past usage** — `max`/`low` never used, `xhigh` once; Harkirat built this system so Claude would choose, so his history is downstream of that advice, not evidence.
- **Flags:** 🔗 bundle-with:\<what\> · 🧩 needs-design · ⛓️ blocked-by/blocks:\<what\>.
- **Combos:** P0/P1+XS/S = quick win (do now / bundle) · P1+L = schedule its own session · P2+XS/S = bundle-only · P3 = ignore till relevant.
- ⚠️ **STANDING RULE, not optional (Harkirat's explicit ask, 2026-08-07 10:22 EDT):** every item gets a model+effort tag from the grid above (`[P· E · Model<ver>-<eff>]`), not just the ones that "feel like" a real build. It's a REFERENCE for whichever session picks the item up, not a binding order — that session judges the model for real once the actual task and its current context are in hand, and may supersede it. **Keep it updated**: if an item's scope, details, or premise change while editing it, re-run the grid and update the tag in the same edit — a stale model tag next to fresh scope is worse than no tag, because it reads as considered when it no longer is. Earlier entries in this file written before this rule (plain `[P0 · S]` with no model) are not retroactively wrong; add the tag the next time that item is touched, don't do a blanket sweep just to backfill it.

Full spec: `reference_priority_tier_system` memory. Canonical copy of this legend: `/Applications/Claude Code/meta-deferred-list.md`.

**Model tags refreshed 2026-07-25 21:43 EDT:** every `Opus4.8-*` tag was rewritten to the equivalent `Opus5-*` — the tier mapping is direct and Opus 4.8 is superseded, so the old tags were pointing at a model Harkirat no longer runs. Effort letters (H/M/L) unchanged.

## 🐞 Active Bugs

- [ ] **The cache-bust stamp check fails on every SQUASH MERGE that touches a mockup asset** `[P1 · S]` (2026-09-05 10:07 EDT)
  - `docs/superpowers/mockups/2026-08-23-portal-interactive/.undeclared.mjs`'s `changedAt()` dates a clean file by **git commit time** (`git log -1 --format=%ct`). On a branch that is correct: the asset is edited, `portal:bust` writes a newer stamp into the eight HTML pages, and the stamp wins. **A squash collapses every branch commit into one**, so the asset's commit time becomes the merge instant — which is always *after* the stamp that was generated mid-branch. The gate then reports the asset as modified after its own stamp and `v3-pre-release` goes red **on a tree that was green as a PR**.
  - Measured on PR #183: green at `fc59b56f`, red at the squash `aef32158` with `docs/superpowers/mockups/2026-08-23-portal-interactive/assets/tokens.css`, `docs/superpowers/mockups/2026-08-23-portal-interactive/assets/app.css` and `docs/superpowers/mockups/2026-08-23-portal-interactive/assets/shell.js` all reported stale against a stamp 17 minutes older than the merge.
  - ⚠️ **The file's own comment anticipates the FRESH-CLONE case and says so** — *"mtime alone could never pass in CI"* — and the squash case is the neighbouring one it does not cover. A re-bust on the integration branch clears it, because that commit touches only the HTML and leaves the assets' commit time behind.
  - **Candidate fixes, none chosen:** compare the stamp against the asset's *content hash* rather than any clock; or have the gate ignore a difference smaller than the merge window; or run `portal:bust` as a post-merge step. **Verify:** squash a branch that edits `assets/*.css` and watch the integration branch's first run.

- [x] **`autofix:safety` failed every morning on every branch, because a test hand-typed a clock time** `[P1 · XS]` (2026-09-05 09:49 EDT)
  - `scripts/autofixSafety.test.mjs`'s case *"a correct stamp for TODAY, beside a placeholder"* hardcoded a fixed HH:MM as the valid stamp, while `.claude/hooks/timestamp-check.sh` — the hook it tests — **denies a future date+time** with a three-minute grace. So the assertion held only from that clock time until midnight local and failed on every run before it, in CI and locally, on any branch. Introduced in `cfcd1d3a`; found 2026-09-05 09:48 EDT when it turned a merge red for a reason that had nothing to do with the merge.
  - 🔴 **It is the project's own standing rule — compute every timestamp, never hand-type one — violated inside a test.** That is the worst place for it: a red suite in the morning and a green one after lunch reads as flakiness rather than as a wrong constant, so the failure teaches a session to dismiss the gate.
  - ✅ **FIXED in the same session** — the stamp is derived as `now − 60s` in the test's own timezone. **Verify:** `node scripts/autofixSafety.test.mjs` passes 6/6 before 11:47 local, which is the window that used to fail.

- **`portal:states` fails in the CI environment on Season's "identity · expanded editor" while passing locally** `[P1 · M · Opus5-Medium]` — filed 2026-09-03 16:18 EDT. **PR #182 was merged past it with the admin override, on Harkirat's explicit decision, against my recommendation of a single re-run.** The failure is unexplained rather than understood, which is why this is P1. Evidence: it passes TWICE locally at the merged tree; it passed once on that branch at 19:51 UTC and failed twice at 19:52; **and it failed on `v3-pre-release` itself after #181 merged**, plus three of four runs on `feat/context-layer-instruments`. So it predates this branch and is environment-dependent, not a regression from it. The tool reports "TWICE, so this is NOT the known race — the subject is genuinely unreachable", and `.identity:not(…)` never matches on the runner. ⚠️ **A required check that is intermittently red trains everyone to override it, and the override is the thing this repo has a retraction on record for (`ebbf196`).** **Do:** reproduce on a runner — compare Chrome version, viewport and step timing against local — then fix the state's reachability or quarantine it explicitly rather than leaving it flaky. **Verify by:** ten consecutive green CI runs on an unrelated branch.

- **[P3 · XS] One unreproduced `timestamp-check.test.sh` failure inside `run-all-tests.sh`, 2026-09-02 22:00 EDT.** The suite reported `35 passed, 1 failed ( timestamp-check.test.sh )` under `TZ=UTC` once, while the same test standalone passed 63/63 in both zones in the same minute, and three immediate re-runs of the full suite came back 36/36. **Do:** if it recurs, capture the failing case name — the runner writes each test's output to `$WORK/<name>.out`, so add a copy-on-failure rather than re-running and hoping. Suspect the parallel `xargs -P` path or a minute-boundary race, not the timezone pin, which is proven both ways. **Verify by:** three consecutive green full-suite runs under `TZ=UTC` are NOT sufficient — that is exactly what was already observed after the failure. Only a captured failing case closes this. ⚠️ Recorded rather than dismissed because a non-reproducible failure in a REQUIRED check is how this repo has been bitten before.


### `[P2 · S]` `codebase-memory-mcp`'s MCP layer drops the parameter its worker requires, and reports it as a crash
*Filed 2026-09-01 20:47 EDT. ⚠️ The first version of this entry said "the indexing worker crashes on a file" and told the next session to bisect the tree for it. **That was the tool's own error message repeated as a diagnosis, and it is wrong** — the bisect would have found nothing, because a different repo entirely fails the same way.*

**The MCP tool `index_repository` takes `project_path`. The worker requires `repo_path`, and the parameter is never forwarded.** The worker exits 1 with the one line `repo_path is required`, and the MCP layer turns that into `{"status":"error","outcome":"exit_nonzero","hint":"Indexing worker crashed on a file. The crash was contained… a future release isolates the culprit file."}` — a message describing a completely different failure.

🔴 **THE COST IS THE MESSAGE, NOT THE BUG.** Chasing it cost a bisect by subtree (every subtree fails, because the parameter is missing regardless), a `pragma integrity_check` (ok), the disk (37 GB free), five stale server processes (killed, irrelevant), the SQLite database (moved aside; the crash predates and outlives it), and a real `.ips` crash report whose stack — `getCellInfo ← sqlite3VdbeExec ← cbm_store_exec ← run_post_extraction` — belongs to an OLDER failure and pointed at SQLite. **Every one of those was a plausible reading of the hint.** A wrong error message is more expensive than a silent one, because it is actionable.

**The workaround, which works today:**

```bash
~/.local/bin/codebase-memory-mcp cli index_repository --repo_path "/Applications/Claude Code/Diors-Builds"
```

Verified 2026-09-01 20:45 EDT: exit 0, `skipped_count: 0`, **9,763 nodes / 20,764 edges**, and `search_graph` then answers for `isStall`, a function written an hour earlier, with `in_degree: 2`. ⚠️ Note the tool ALSO renamed its argument between layers — `cli` takes `--repo_path`, the MCP tool takes `project_path`, and `detect_changes`/`search_graph` take `project` (a NAME, not a path). Three spellings for one idea.

⚠️ **`detect_changes` after a fresh index reports EVERY file as changed** (620 here) because the rebuild leaves `base_sha` empty. That is not staleness, and reading it as staleness is how this entry got its first wrong diagnosis. Check `list_projects`'s `base_sha` before believing a change count.

⚠️ **Two artifacts left on disk deliberately, not cleaned up**: `~/.cache/codebase-memory-mcp/*.moved-1788309827` (the previous database and a `.db.corrupt` the server had already rotated) are kept so the state is recoverable. Delete them once a week of clean indexing has passed.

**Report upstream:** the MCP handler should forward `project_path` as the worker's `repo_path`, and `index.supervisor.worker_failed` should surface the worker log's last line — it already writes the path (`~/.cache/codebase-memory-mcp/logs/.worker-<pid>.log`) into its own `warn` line and then discards the content.

**Verify:** `index_repository` succeeds through the MCP tool with `project_path`, without the CLI workaround.

### `[P2 · M]` `portal:states` is non-deterministic, and it now blocks a §L ④ claim
*Filed 2026-09-01 20:01 EDT by the Access session, which could not claim its machine floor because of it.*

Measured on `feat/access-portal-conformance` at `c41e8e0`, a tree whose only portal changes are Access-scoped:
- **In-suite**: failed on `identity · closed again from the header's dead space` — *"nothing matched `.identity.collapsed .srec-grid` within 5000ms"*.
- **Standalone, immediately after**: failed on a **different** state, `composer · the paste box` — *"nothing matches `.nwhost .nw-paste` within 12s"*.

🔴 **NEWER EVIDENCE 2026-09-02 22:04 EDT — THE INSTRUMENT'S OWN 'TWICE MEANS GENUINELY UNREACHABLE' RULE IS FALSIFIED, AND THIS IS THE FINDING THAT MATTERS.** PR #181 changed `scripts/portalStates.mjs` itself, which the standdown correctly treats as RUN — so the walk executed and stalled **twice** on Season's `identity · closed again from the header's dead space`, waiting for `.identity.collapsed .srec-grid`. `scripts/portalStates.mjs` prints *"TWICE, so this is NOT the known race — the subject is genuinely unreachable"* and fails the suite on that basis. **It is reachable.** `TEST_CACHE=0 node scripts/portalStates.mjs --realm season` walked all 21 states green on this Mac minutes later, that state included. ⚠️ **So the retry does not discriminate what it claims to discriminate**: a second attempt separates a race from an unreachable subject only when the two attempts differ in timing, and on a uniformly slow 2-core runner they do not — the step clicks before mount both times. The classification is calibrated on a machine fast enough for the retry to mean something. **Do:** either make the retry vary something real (a longer pre-step settle on the second attempt, not just a repeat), or have `--ci` report a twice-stall as UNRESOLVED rather than as a proven defect. ⚠️ **Raising the deadline is already ruled out by that file's own record** — 4000 → 12000 → 45000, three raises, still failing; do not reach for a fourth. **Verify by:** a twice-stall on CI whose state then fails locally too. That has never yet happened.

🔴 **NEW EVIDENCE 2026-09-02 17:13 EDT🔴 **NEW EVIDENCE 2026-09-02 17:13 EDT — IT NOW BLOCKS A BRANCH WITH NO PORTAL FILES AT ALL, AND IT IS NOT ALTERNATING.** PR #181 (`feat/context-layer-instruments`) changes 31 files: hooks, rules, docs, `scripts/docs-audit.mjs` and a since-deleted `rulesBudget` script. **Zero portal files. Zero runtime `.js`.** It failed CI **three consecutive runs**, every one on `manifest · nothing matches the search` — *"nothing matches `#manifest .empty` within 45s"* — while `TEST_CACHE=0 node scripts/portalStates.mjs --ci` passes **locally on that same tree**, 44 states walked, exit 0.

⚠️ **That is a DIFFERENT signature from the one recorded below, and a more useful one.** This entry records *"red, standalone green, green, red on a different state"* — a coin flip on one tree. Three-for-three in CI while green locally is not a coin flip: it points at the RUNNER being slow enough to blow a fixed selector budget the Mac never approaches, rather than at a race. The instrument even prints *"TWICE, so this is NOT the known race — the subject is genuinely unreachable"*, which is the check asserting the opposite of what the local run demonstrates.

⚠️ **And the consequence is structural now, not cosmetic.** `syntax-check` is a REQUIRED check, so a non-deterministic portal instrument can block any PR in the repo, including one whose diff cannot have caused it. **That coupling is worth deciding separately from the root cause**: either the budget becomes environment-aware, or `portal:states` stops gating a PR whose diff contains no portal file. ⚠️ CI has no `workflow_dispatch`, so the base branch could not be re-tested without publishing a throwaway branch — that discriminator was not available.

Both selectors are emitted by `portal/ui/season.js` and `portal/ui/composer.js`, neither of which this branch touches; its five changed files are `portal/api/access.js`, `portal/ui/access.js`, `portal/ui/app.css` and two fixtures, and its three CSS selector changes (`.mx .mxcell`, `.mxcell[aria-checked=true]:not(.inh)`, `#manifest,#sessions`) are unreachable from Season. **This matches the signature §L already records** — *"red, standalone green, green, red on a different state"* over four runs on one unchanged tree.

🔴 **The consequence is not cosmetic: §L condition ④ cannot honestly be claimed on any branch while this holds**, because the rule is that a standalone pass is what distinguishes the flake from a real defect, and a standalone FAILURE on a different state distinguishes nothing. Two realms have now shipped with ④ measured against a run that did not correspond to their final tree.

**What to do:** make the failure attributable rather than chasing the timeout. Either (a) have `portalStates` retry a stalled subject once and report `FLAKED` distinctly from `FAILED`, so a suite result separates the two, or (b) find the shared cause — both failures are a subject that never appears after its steps ran, which points at the step sequence racing the render rather than at the two states.

🔴 **AND IT MASKED A REAL FAILURE, WHICH IS THE PART THAT ACTUALLY COSTS.** `npm test` is one `&&` chain: `portalStates` runs before `portalHarness.test.js`, so the local suite died at the flake and **never reached** a genuine defect this branch had introduced — `/api/access` promised a `sessionTtlHours` key the harness stub did not serve. CI found it, because CI ran the same chain against a tree where the flake happened not to fire. **So the cost is not a wasted re-run: a non-deterministic early gate silently truncates every gate after it, and the suite reports the flake's name rather than the defect's.** That is why the fix must classify rather than retry.

**Verify:** five consecutive `node scripts/portalStates.mjs --ci` runs on one unchanged tree, all green — or a `FLAKED` classification that a suite can pass through while still naming what stalled.




- `[P2 · S · Sonnet5-High]` **`.stt.saved`'s ink is a FIXED near-black where the design derives it per surface — a live contrast hazard on any realm passing a dark accent.** *Found 2026-09-01 by self-review after fixing the light-accent half of the same rule.* `portal/ui/app.css`'s `.stt.saved[style*="--c"]{color:var(--ci,var(--on-accent))}` falls back to `--on-accent` (#07090A) whenever `--ci` is unset. The design does not have this problem because `docs/superpowers/mockups/2026-08-23-portal-interactive/assets/shell.js` computes `inkOn()` from each chip's COMPUTED background at runtime, choosing black or white per surface — its own COMPANION §5.9.5 says a threshold is a guess and both ratios must be computed. **`portal/ui/manifest.js`'s `StatePill` only computes `--ci` when the realm passes a `var(--token)` accent** (`/^var\((--[\w-]+)\)$/`), so `season.js` passing a raw `row.accentHex` yields `ink=''` and the chip takes near-black on an arbitrary stored colour. COMPANION measured exactly this shape at **2.86:1** on a draw window's plum. ⚠️ **Not introduced by the 2026-09-01 fix** — that touched only the no-inline-`--c` path, where the fill is always `--ok` and near-black is right at 11.29:1. **Do:** make `inkOnTopic` accept a hex literal, or compute the ink where the accent is chosen. **Verify by:** a `portalContrastRendered` case asserting every `.stt` clears 4.5:1 against its own computed background on Season with a dark topic.
- `[P3 · S · Sonnet5-Medium]` **A time-limited directive in a permanent carrier has no expiry and nothing evaluates one — it went wrong in the highest-traffic file in the repo.** *Filed 2026-09-01.* `docs/SESSION-START.md` is injected into EVERY session and carried *"🔴 A REALM AUDIT IS NOT THE FIRST COMMAND ANY MORE"* plus *"Task 11 is THE MERGE, which is in flight as PR #177"*. Both were true for about a day; `gh pr view 177` returns MERGED. The block even says *"Delete this whole block when the pass ends"* — **an instruction addressed to a reader who has no way to know the pass ended and no reason to check.** The class is general: every "remove this later" note anywhere. **Do:** an `expires_when:` key in front matter or in the block, checked by `docs-audit`, holding a command whose exit code decides — here `gh pr view 177 --json state -q .state` returning `MERGED`. `docs-audit` already runs on every PR and already parses front matter. **Verify by:** planting a block whose condition is already met and seeing the audit fail.
- `[P2 · S · Sonnet5-High]` **Nothing couples a §L row's tick to the instruments that condition actually names — and the receipt board that could is already written and unread.** *Filed 2026-09-01; named by a cold reader as "the most expensive mistake available".* §L's close conditions ① and ⑤ name `portalConverge` and a real-server pass. `scripts/portalStatus.mjs` already prints, per realm, whether each of `audit · inventory · diff · converge · realwalk` has run against the current `portal/ui` — **but nothing reads that board before a row is ticked, and the failure it predicts has already happened twice**: Broadcast's ① was written from `portal:audit`'s ① CASCADE section while the board read `converge · never` in the same tree, and Armory was one summary from being closed with `portal:inventory` never run on it. 🔴 **The expensive shape is closing a realm on the harness alone** — mockup and harness are both fixture-driven and corroborate each other vacuously (§0.2), so a defect only real data can show (Access's `.mxgrp` colspans mis-grouping when a command is appended) survives a green close. **Do:** `portalStatus --assert <realm>` exiting non-zero when any of the five reads `never` or is behind HEAD, called before a §L row is ticked; or a commit-time warn on a plan edit that ticks a row whose receipts are stale. **Verify by:** deleting `local/.portal-receipts/<realm>.converge.json` and seeing the assert fail.
- `[P2 · S · Sonnet5-Medium]` **§L's "update this table in the SAME COMMIT" is the plan's self-declared load-bearing rule and nothing checks it — it has already failed once.** *Filed 2026-09-01 after a cold reader found it.* `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-27-portal-conformance.md`'s §L opens with *"🔴 THE ONE RULE THAT KEEPS THIS DOCUMENT TRUE: update this table in the SAME COMMIT that closes a unit… A row whose status is wrong is worse than no row, because the next session trusts it."* **The failure it predicted then happened**: §L row 4 carried `47.7KB · 7 handlers · 15 data-attrs` for Access — all three wrong, one of them a figure no instrument produces — while `ACCESS-PROMPT.md` documented the disagreement in the same tree. **Do:** a `PreToolUse` hook on `git commit` that warns (never denies — Harkirat's standing constraint) when the staged diff touches `portal/ui/**` or `portal/fixtures/**` and does not touch the plan. ⚠️ **Write its `.test.sh` in the same change** — every script in `.claude/hooks/` needs one and `run-all-tests.sh` computes coverage from disk. **Verify by:** staging a `portal/ui` change alone and seeing the warning, then staging it with a plan edit and seeing silence.
- `[P1 · S · Sonnet5-High]` 🔴 **Season's recorded conformance figures do NOT reproduce at HEAD — the default view measures 12.0% / 135 regions / +224px against a §L row that says 0.2%.** *Measured 2026-09-01 during Broadcast's pass.* 🔴 **AND `portal:status` CAN NO LONGER SURFACE THIS, WHICH MAKES THE ENTRY MORE IMPORTANT RATHER THAN LESS.** It flagged season `🔴 2 — RE-MEASURE` (fixture stamped `56ff4cc`) when this was filed; the Broadcast pass then re-recorded season's geometry fixture at `484b846` as §0.5b requires after a shared-surface change, so the board now reads **`✅ fresh`** for a realm whose diff is five thousandths of what its §L row claims. **A geometry fixture records that the page has not MOVED, never that it is RIGHT** — the board's own footnote says so, and this is the first case where the two readings point opposite ways. ⚠️ **This is NOT Broadcast's doing and that was checked rather than assumed**: the same three realms were diffed with this branch's changes stashed and restored, and season read 12.0% / 135 / +224px both times, armory 8.6% / 893 / +508px both times. So the drift landed in one of the two `portal/ui` commits between the recording and HEAD. **Do:** diff season at HEAD, find which commit moved it, and either fix or cite the new regions — its §L row currently states a number a reader would trust. **Verify by:** §L row 1's figures matching a fresh `portalDiff` run, or the row naming the new floor with its cited set.
- `[P2 · S · Sonnet5-Medium]` **Broadcast's Post drawer takes an EXPIRY DATE where the design takes "blank, days, or never".** *Found 2026-09-01 porting the form into the design's drawer.* `broadcast.html:421` renders `Expires in` as a text input parsed by `parseExpiry` — blank means the 60-day default, a whole number means days, and the literal word `never` means no expiry at all. `portal/ui/broadcast.js`'s form uses `<input type="date">`, which **cannot express "never"** — the single state this whole realm exists to make visible, and the subject of its own HeadsUp callout. The container was ported in this pass; the input was not, because the payload shape belongs to `core/ops/announcements.js` and §0.6b puts the operation algebra out of a conformance pass. **Verify by:** the drawer accepting `never` and the staged op carrying a null expiry that the op layer reads as no-expiry rather than as the default.

The design has no previewed-row concept at all, so a rule for it is a small design decision, not a port. **Verify by:** clicking an Armory row visibly marking it, or the class being removed.

The disproof sat fourteen lines above it in the same file. Corrected. ⚠️ **The remedy remains a re-run, and the cost remains the judgement it forces**, which is what this entry has always said. Originally: `[P1 · S · Sonnet5-High]` 🔴 **`portal:states` is NON-DETERMINISTIC LOCALLY — measured 1 fail / 1 pass / 1 fail across three consecutive `--ci` runs, a DIFFERENT state failing each time.** *Filed 2026-08-31 19:2x EDT.* Observed states: `identity · closed again from the header's dead space` · `default · home` · `one-way panel · tier 3` · `command bar open` · `one-way · every row present and legible without the capability` · **`view · Repairs`** (added 2026-09-01: *"nothing matches `.repwrap .repgrp .repnote` within 12s"* in a full `npm test`, then **exit 0 with all 40 states** running `portalStates.mjs --ci` alone thirty seconds later — the selector lives in `track.js`, which that session never touched). 🔴 **MEASURED PROPERLY 2026-09-01 16:36 EDT, FOUR CONSECUTIVE RUNS ON ONE UNCHANGED TREE: red (`view · Repairs`, `.repwrap .repgrp .repnote`) → standalone `portalStates.mjs --ci` **green, all 40 states** → in-suite **green** → in-suite red again on a DIFFERENT state (`export strip open`, `.exs`). So it is ~50% in-suite and the failing state changes every time — which is the entry's own predicted signature, now with a run count behind it.** ⚠️ **CORRECTED the same day, on the merged tree: `--ci` ALSO fails standalone.** Measured immediately after #178 landed — one red, then three consecutive greens, so roughly **1 in 5 standalone** against ~1 in 2 in-suite. 🔴 **This matters more than the ratio: an earlier version of this entry said "standalone-green", and a session reading that would treat a standalone pass as proof its change is innocent — or a standalone FAILURE as proof it is guilty. Neither follows.** The only sound test is the one already stated below: does the failing selector belong to anything you edited. Load is a factor, not the cause. 🔴 **THE COST IS NOT THE RED RUN — IT IS THE JUDGEMENT IT FORCES.** Every session that hits this must decide, live, whether the red is its own defect, and the only way to decide is to re-run the state alone and reason about whether the selector belongs to anything it edited. That is a two-call detour and a real chance of a wrong call in both directions: dismissing a genuine break as the flake, or hunting a ghost. The message is always the same shape — *"did not reach its own subject — nothing matches `<sel>` after its steps ran"* — which is a TIMING failure, not a logic one: the step ran, the DOM had not settled, the selector missed. ⚠️ **It is in the `npm test` chain, so roughly half of all local suite runs are red for a reason unrelated to the change under test.** That is worse than a broken gate, because it trains a session to re-run until green and then believe the green — and this session did exactly that twice before measuring it. 🔴 **CI does NOT reproduce it** (PR #176's `syntax-check` failed only on the timezone assertions and passed `portal:states`), so a green CI is not evidence the flake is gone, and a red local run is not evidence of a real defect. **Both readings are currently unreliable in opposite directions.** ⚠️ **`feat/portal-redesign-session-b` touches BOTH `scripts/portalStates.mjs` and `portal/ui/oneway.js`**, so "pre-existing" is NOT established — that needs checking against `origin/v3-pre-release` before it is claimed. A related fix landed 2026-08-30 (*"fixed non-deterministic portalStates test (700ms sleep)"*), so a fixed-timeout remedy has already been tried once and is evidently insufficient.
  **What to do:** replace the fixed settle delay with a real wait-for-selector that polls until the subject appears or a deadline passes, and make the failure message print what the DOM *did* contain so a miss is diagnosable rather than just reported.

- `[P2 · XS · Sonnet5-Medium · 🔗bundle:portal-conformance]` **The portal has ZERO `transition` declarations, but the mockups specify one.** *Found 2026-08-30 12:00 EDT by diffing declared CSS properties between `docs/superpowers/mockups/2026-08-20-portal/` and `portal/ui/`, during the skills/tools review.* `01-season-spine.html:82` and `03-three-surfaces.html:72` both carry `transition:filter .12s` (the second adds `box-shadow .12s`) on the interactive bar; `rg 'transition' portal/` returns nothing across all 240 lines of portal CSS. **The overlay cannot see this** — it is a pixel diff at a frozen clock, so a missing transition is invisible to it and Season closed at 0.1–0.2% with the property absent. ⚠️ Note the mockups otherwise set `transition:none!important`/`animation:none!important` deliberately, so motion was suppressed *to make the diff deterministic*; this one survived that suppression and is therefore a real specification, not a leftover. - `[P1 · S · Opus5-Medium · 🧩needs-design]` **The overlay measures rendered pixels and therefore cannot see a whole CLASS of mockup–portal divergence: declared CSS properties that produce no pixels at a frozen clock.** *Filed 2026-08-30 12:00 EDT; the two bugs above are its first two instances, and both were found by hand because nothing automated could.* Blind to at least: transitions/animations, `--custom-properties` that are declared but unused, `:focus-visible` and other state-only rules, loading/skeleton states, and anything whose difference is temporal. **This is the third recorded instance of the same failure family** — see memory `feedback_instruments_inherit_their_authors_blind_spot` (the page diff that read 888px of 4,378) and the already-filed "the overlay never clicks" gap, which is its sibling. **Proposed instrument:** a property-level diff — parse the declared CSS of a mockup and of the corresponding portal stylesheet, compare the property sets per selector, and report properties present in one and absent in the other. It would have caught both bugs above in a single run and keeps catching the class. ⚠️ **AND IT WOULD ALSO HAVE CAUGHT THE SOURCE ERROR ABOVE**, because a property diff names the two files it compared — which is the discipline the two bugs were missing. 🟡 **PARTLY CLOSED THE SAME DAY:** ④ STYLE now samples **47** computed properties (was 27), adding `boxShadow`, `backgroundImage`, `borderStyle` and the outline set, plus `transitionProperty`/`transitionDuration` — the audit does NOT zero transitions the way the pixel diff does, so a declared-but-motionless transition IS now visible to it. What remains genuinely uncovered, and what this filing should still build: a token declared and never used, and any selector present in one sheet and absent in the other at PROPERTY level rather than selector level. ⚠️ It must state its own coverage in its output, per that memory's rule, or it becomes the next instrument with an unexamined frame.
- `[P2 · XS · Sonnet5-Medium]` ~~**`npm test` prints "Run with TEST_CACHE=0 to force a real re-run", and doing exactly that makes the suite FAIL — on an unrelated file.**~~ ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-25 21:0x EDT** (`7867c89`). `testCache.test.mjs` neutralised `CI` in its three spawn sites but not `TEST_CACHE`, and `testCache.mjs` reads `TEST_CACHE === "0" || CI === "true"` — the author saw the hazard and fixed half of it. Now **15/15 in all three modes**: plain, `TEST_CACHE=0`, `CI=true`. Only running the advertised command would ever have shown it. *Found 2026-08-25 00:5x EDT while forcing a real hook run during a completeness sweep.*

  `scripts/testCache.test.mjs` inherits `TEST_CACHE=0` from the environment, so the seven cases that assert a cache HIT (`warm run … skips re-execution`, `changed input invalidates the cache`, `changed command …`, `TEST_CACHE=0 forces a real run despite a valid cache`, `CI=true forces a real run …`, and both directory-input cases) all take the bypass path instead and fail. **8 passed, 7 failed.** Run on its own with no flag it is **15 passed, 0 failed**, so the suite is correct and only the inherited environment is wrong.

  **Why it matters more than a flaky test:** the banner the cache prints is the *documented* way to get an observed pass rather than a cached one, and this repo's own memory records "cached greens" as a real failure mode. Following the printed instruction produces a red suite for a reason unrelated to your change — which teaches the next session either to chase a phantom or to stop believing red. It also **truncates the run**: `npm test` is one `&&` chain, so it dies at `testCache.test.mjs` and never reaches `test:hooks`, which is the thing you were trying to force in the first place.

  **Fix:** `testCache.test.mjs` should neutralise the ambient flag for its own cache-hit cases — read it once at startup, then `delete process.env.TEST_CACHE` (or spawn each case with an explicit env) so the cases control the variable rather than inherit it. ⚠️ Do **not** "fix" it by dropping the `TEST_CACHE=0` case, which is the one that proves the bypass works.

  **Workaround until then:** `TEST_CACHE=0 npm run test:hooks` runs the hook suite uncached directly, bypassing the chain — verified 2026-08-25, **29 passed, 0 failed**.

  **Verify by:** `TEST_CACHE=0 npm test` exiting 0, with `testCache.test.mjs` still reporting 15 passed and the chain reaching `test:hooks`.

Measured across all eight pages: Season goes 1 → 0 (the NOW pulse) and the rest hold at 0. It carries its own vacuity guard — if no reduced-motion rules are found at all it reports that rather than passing on an empty set.

  ⏳ **What remains is narrower than it was:** nothing has rendered the portal *under* the preference itself. The chrome-devtools MCP's `emulate` tool exposes viewport, colour scheme, network and CPU — **not `prefers-reduced-motion`** — so the DevTools route named in the previous handoff was not actually available. What is proven is a **necessary condition** (every animating selector has an override), not that the overrides produce a still page.

  ⚠️ **Do not read rule 14 as closing this.** It asserts coverage, which is a relationship between two sets of selectors; it cannot see an override that is present and wrong (a `transform` left animating, a keyframe still moving something the override does not touch).

  **What is left to prove is only the GATING** — that the media query switches the rules on for someone whose OS says reduce. Rules-apply is proven, coverage is proven; the query itself is not. **How to close it:** drive Chrome with `Emulation.setEmulatedMedia({features:[{name:'prefers-reduced-motion',value:'reduce'}]})` over raw CDP (Playwright's `page.emulateMedia({reducedMotion:'reduce'})` is the one-liner) and re-run PASS 4f **without** the lift — if it still reports zero running animations, the query gates correctly. **Verify by:** that variant passing on all eight pages.

  ⚠️ **Do not read the three together as one green.** Coverage (rule 14), effect (PASS 4f) and gating (unproven) are three separate claims, and this entry stays open because the third is missing — not because the first two are in doubt.

- 🚫 **NOT A BUG — CLOSED 2026-08-24 as SUPERSEDED BY DESIGN. Do not implement this entry; following it would undo shipped work.** *(Filed 2026-08-23 20:45 EDT, when the Track having five lanes was still the design.)* Patch notes were **deliberately removed from the Track** on 2026-08-24 and given their own instrument. A patch note is a **publication**, not a state with a duration: `models/SeasonalData.js`'s `patchNotes[]` subdocument gives it a `releaseDate` and no end, and `isEventEnded()` returns false for it forever, so it does not belong on an axis whose every other lane answers "when is this ON?". It now renders as the **Season Record**, a vertical rail beneath the Track (Harkirat picked that from three built variants), and it keeps its row in the Manifest and its chip in the legend. See COMPANION §5.9d. ⚠️ **This entry is left here rather than deleted** because the conservation rule says an item leaves an active list only by appearing in its archive — and because a prescribed fix that would reverse a design decision is worth a tombstone, not a silent removal.

Two real approaches, not yet decided between: **(a)** capture `username` at login time and store it on `PortalSession` (needs a schema change *and* a `docs/legal/PRIVACY.md` §2/Appendix A update in the same change, per this repo's own privacy-model-coverage check), or **(b)** resolve id→username live via a Discord API call at render time (adds a dependency the portal doesn't currently have — it has no gateway connection, per `portal/api/analytics.js`'s own header comment about why bot-side stats can't be read directly). **Verify by:** whichever is picked, confirm the shell's identity chip reads a real username instead of a raw id on next login.

- `[P3 · XS · Sonnet5-Medium]` **`core/ops/announcements.js`'s `validatePost` doesn't actually reject a `startsAt` that's after `expiresAt` when both arrive as JSON strings (the portal's real shape).** *Found live 2026-08-21 23:32 EDT verifying the portal's Broadcast realm (`feat/portal-operation-core`) — staged and committed `announcement.post` with `startsAt: '2026-09-15'` and `expiresAt: '2026-09-01T00:00:00.000Z'` (start after expiry) and it was accepted, not rejected.* The guard (`if (startsAt && expiresAt && startsAt > expiresAt)`) compares a real `Date` (parsed from `startsAt`) against `expiresAt`, which stays a raw STRING on the "already normalized" fast path (`alreadyNormalized()` only checks the key is present, not its type) — `>` between a Date and a non-numeric string coerces the string via `ToPrimitive`/`ToNumber`, which is `NaN`, so the comparison is always `false`. Not a data-integrity bug (Mongoose still casts the string to a real Date on save), just a missing validation guard for a nonsensical admin input. Fix is either converting `expiresAt` to a `Date` before the comparison when it arrives as a string, or asserting both sides are the same type before comparing.

- `[P3 · XS · Sonnet5-Medium]` **`timestamp-check.test.sh`'s "just-outside tolerance" case is TIME-FLAKY — it fails intermittently with no code change.** *Filed 2026-08-15 17:20 EDT, hit live during an unrelated `npm test`.*

  The case builds a stamp intended to sit just beyond the 180-second tolerance and asserts the hook denies it. Because the stamp is computed relative to *now* and the hook re-reads the clock a moment later, the two can land on opposite sides of the boundary depending on execution timing — the assertion then gets `SILENT` and the whole suite exits 1. Observed failing once, then passing on three consecutive re-runs with **zero** modifications to `timestamp-check.sh` or its test (`git diff HEAD` on both files was empty).

  **Why it matters more than a one-line flake normally would:** `npm test` is a CI gate and a pre-merge gate here. A suite that fails for a reason unrelated to the change teaches everyone to re-run it and move on — and re-running until green is exactly how a *real* failure gets waved through. This repo already has that lesson recorded about the timestamp gate specifically.

  **Fix:** make the boundary deterministic rather than racing the clock — freeze the reference time the case computes against (inject it, or pick an offset far enough outside the window that scheduling jitter cannot cross it, e.g. tolerance + 60s rather than tolerance + a second or two). ⚠️ Do **not** widen the hook's tolerance to make the test pass; the tolerance is the product behaviour and the test is what is wrong.

  **Verify by:** running `bash .claude/hooks/timestamp-check.test.sh` 20 times in a loop and getting 20 clean exits.

- `[P1 · M · Opus5-Medium]` **In a WORKTREE session, every `${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}`-based hook inspects the WORKTREE — not the tree the work is actually happening in — and its silence reads as all-clear.** *Filed 2026-08-15 17:11 EDT, proven live.*

  27 of the registered hooks resolve their root as `${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR:-/Applications/Claude Code/Diors-Builds}` and then `cd` there to read git state. In a worktree session the harness sets that to the **worktree**. If the session is actually editing the main repo — which is the normal shape, since a worktree session that needs the real tree just `cd`s to it — then every one of those gates checks a tree with none of the work in it, finds nothing, and stays quiet. **A gate that inspects the wrong tree is indistinguishable from a gate that passed.**

  **Proof (not inference).** `untracked-doc-guard.sh`, same stdin, only the env differing: `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR=<main repo>` → fires, correctly naming the untracked a temporary untracked probe file under `docs/reference/`; `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR=<worktree>` → silent, because the worktree has 0 untracked `.md`. The live session was silent, which is how the session's own resolution was identified.

  **This also explains the inconsistency** that made it hard to spot: hooks carrying a **hardcoded** `cd "/Applications/Claude Code/Diors-Builds"` (release-ready-check, squash-trailer-gate) fired correctly all session, while `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`-based ones did not. So the gate set was *partially* live, which reads as "hooks are working" rather than "half of them are looking somewhere else."

  ⚠️ **Do NOT fix this by hardcoding the path everywhere** — that breaks any remote/CI/non-Mac session, which is exactly the `[P1 · XS]` `git tag -a` bug already in this list. The two failure modes pull in opposite directions and a fix has to satisfy both.

  ⚠️ **A SECOND, HARDER SYMPTOM — observed 2026-08-15 17:33 EDT, same root cause.** Once the worktree is *deleted*, the same resolution stops being merely wrong and becomes unreachable: `${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}/.claude/hooks/*.sh` now points into a directory that no longer exists, so **`outstanding-not-filed.sh` and `completeness-sweep.sh` could not run at all** for the rest of that session, and their registrations fall through to a hard `decision:"block"`. **Both symptoms present identically from the outside — silence.** A gate reading the wrong tree, a gate that cannot be read, and a gate that genuinely found nothing are indistinguishable without checking, which is the whole reason this is P1 rather than a tidiness item. *(Worked around in-session with a gitignored symlink from the stale worktree path back to the repo root — that restores every `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`-based hook at once and is worth knowing as a stopgap, but it is a session-local shim and not the fix.)*

  ✅ **THE DECISION IS MADE — Harkirat, 2026-08-23 13:30 EDT.** A hook resolves the tree it governs from the **git toplevel of the path being acted on**, falling back to `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`, then to the repo root. The entry's own suggested answer was the one chosen, so this is no longer a question and the entry is **not** blocked on anyone. The resolver, its six-case test, and the before/after differential across all 27 call sites are carried in `/Applications/Claude Code/2026-08-23-workflow-compliance-plan.md` **Tasks 2–3**. ⚠️ *This sentence read "The open decision is Harkirat's, which is why this is filed rather than fixed" until 2026-08-24 — false for a day, and it made unblocked work read as parked. Everything else in this entry (the proof, both symptoms, the verify-by step) is unchanged and still accurate.*

  **Verify a fix by:** running the two-env probe above and confirming both resolutions report the tree that actually holds the change; then a real worktree session that edits the main repo and sees the git-based gates fire.

- `[P2 · S · Sonnet5-Medium]` **The `gh pr merge` hook matchers fire on documentation TEXT, because `grep` is line-oriented and the matcher is line-anchored.** *Filed 2026-08-15 15:38 EDT, hit live while writing `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-15-draw-cost-calculator.md`.*

  Every one of these hooks matches with `grep -qE '(^|[;&|] *)((rtk|sudo|command|nohup|time) +)*gh pr merge'` against the whole of `.tool_input.command`. `grep` evaluates that pattern **per line**, so `^` anchors at the start of *any* line in the payload — not at the start of the command. A `cat > file <<'EOF'` heredoc whose body contains a line beginning with those three words therefore trips the gate, and `release-ready-check.sh` **hard-blocks the write**. Nothing is being merged; a plan document is merely describing how to merge.

  **Repro:** run a heredoc write whose body contains a line starting with `gh pr merge`. Observed: `RELEASE NOT READY — … CHANGELOG-SUMMARY.md has no line`, and the file is never created.

  **Workaround used (not a fix):** wrote the file with the `Write` tool instead of Bash. That works because these are `Bash` `PreToolUse`/`PostToolUse` matchers, but it silently removes the whole payload from every other Bash-level gate too, so it is a bypass rather than a remedy.

  **Real fix:** strip heredoc bodies before matching, so the gates test the command being *invoked* rather than arbitrary text being *written*. Something like deleting everything between a `<<'?EOF'?` opener and its closing delimiter, then matching the remainder. ⚠️ Do **not** "fix" this by dropping the `^` anchor — that widens the hole to every prose mention anywhere in a command. And note this affects all five `gh pr merge` matchers in `.claude/settings.json` plus `release-ready-check.sh`, not just the one that blocked; fixing one leaves the rest wrong. The blocking one is the only P2 — the other four are advisory `additionalContext`, so they merely emit a spurious reminder.

- `[P1 · XS · Sonnet5-Medium]` **The `git tag -a` release-tag-invariant `PreToolUse` hook hard-errors instead of skipping on any non-Mac session.** *Filed 2026-08-07 08:06 EDT, hit live tagging v2.58.0.*

  The hook (matches `git tag +-a +v[0-9]`) unconditionally `cd`s to `/Applications/Claude Code/ Diors-Builds` — Harkirat's own Mac path — before it can cross-check the tag commit's `package.json` version. In any remote/Linux sandbox that path doesn't exist, the `cd` fails, the `&&` chain short-circuits, and instead of the intended silent pass-through it surfaces as `PreToolUse:Bash hook error: ... No stderr output` — **every retry of the identical command fails the same way**, deterministically blocking the tool call.

  **Workaround used that session (not a fix):** `git tag --annotate v2.58.0 -m "..." <sha>` instead of `-a` — mechanically identical annotated tag, just doesn't match the hook's trigger regex. Only reached for after independently re-verifying the exact invariant the hook protects (`git show <sha>:package.json` matched the tag name) — a workaround for a non-functional check, not a bypass of a working one.

  **Real fix:** give the hook a graceful missing-path fallback (skip silently / report SKIPPED), the same pattern `docs-audit.mjs`'s `external-anchors`/`memory-slug` checks already use correctly when Harkirat's local paths aren't present. Will hit **every** future remote/non-Mac session that tries to tag a release, not just this one.

- `[P3 · XS]` **`branch-discipline-guard.sh` does not catch a commit whose verb sits inside quotes** — e.g. `bash -c "git commit -m x"`, `sh -c '… git commit …'`. *Filed 2026-08-06 21:44 EDT.* The matcher requires `^` or `;&|` immediately before `git`, and in these forms a quote precedes it. **Pre-existing, not a regression** — it has never been caught. Found by writing a test that ASSERTED it was caught; the test failed and the belief was wrong, not the code. `branch-discipline-guard.test.sh` now pins the current behaviour (`"bash -c quoted commit NOT caught"`) so a fix has something to flip. ⚠️ **Do NOT "fix" this by stripping double-quoted spans** — that widens the hole rather than closing it, because `-m "message"` text would stop being scanned too. The real fix is to also match `git commit` after a quote character, without matching prose. **Low priority:** every real commit path in this project is a bare `git commit`, and the Write/Edit half of the guard still blocks the edits that would precede one.

- `[P3 · S]` **`patchnotes.js`'s media carousel has NO component-count chunking, and nothing has ever tested it at scale.** *Moved here 2026-08-06 08:13 EDT from `docs/reference/platform-constraints.md` back when it was still called **known-issues** — it was split so the file could honestly take the new name. (The old name is written without its `.md` on purpose: `xref` matches path-shaped references and cannot tell a historical mention from a live pointer, so spelling it as a path would fail CI.) This entry is the clearest thing in it that was never a platform constraint.*

  `calendar.js` and `draws.js` both carry defensive chunking; `patchnotes.js` does not. Components V2 caps a message at **40 components counted recursively**, and exceeding it is a real production crash this project has already hit once (see the platform cheat-sheet in `CLAUDE.md`).

  ⚠️ **Filed as a bug rather than a constraint, but stated honestly: it is UNVERIFIED, not confirmed broken.** The original wording was "likely fine since patch note screenshots per entry are usually few, but not empirically verified the way draws/calendar chunking was" — and that is still exactly the state. It sits here because the thing at fault would be *our* missing guard, not a Discord limit; leaving it in a constraints file would have implied the opposite.
  **Cheapest close:** count the components a worst-case real patch-note entry renders, rather than building chunking speculatively. If it is nowhere near 40, this becomes a not-a-real-issue entry in the archive and costs nothing further. ⇄ Also named in the 🧹 Someday "General housekeeping session" item ("decide `/patch notes` carousel component-count chunking"); that line is the horizon, this is the detail.

- `[P2 · M]` 🧩 **The mobile nav's liquid indicator still artefacts in LIGHT mode while the birth animation plays — on iOS only.** *Filed 2026-08-04 13:02 EDT at Harkirat's call to defer it; found and chased across the mobile-polish session.*

  **Symptom:** a saturated rectangle fills the tab strip for the ~2.4s the birth runs, then goes. Dark mode is clean. Reproduced repeatedly on Harkirat's iPhone; **not reproducible in Chrome or in the preview pane**, so this needs a real device or CDP against a WebKit build.

  **The colour is the diagnostic and should be the first thing checked after any change.** It paints `invert(accent)` — lime on the violet License page (`invert(#9B6BE3)` = `#64941C`), yellow on citron Contributors, black on Terms. That is the **accent plate** (`.mtint`) coming out inverted, and the plate is a *sibling* of the filtered element (`.mgo`), not a child. A filter reaching a sibling it does not own means the light chain is **composited** wrongly, not merely blended wrongly.

  ⚠️ **Four fixes are already spent. Do not restart at the top of this list** — each moved *when* the artefact appeared without removing it, and that progression is the useful evidence:
  1. Removed `-webkit-overflow-scrolling` from `.mstrip`, promoted `.mbar` with `translateZ(0)` — no effect. The promotion was reverted; the prefix removal was kept on its own merits.
  2. Retired the layer after its birth (`.mgw.spent`, `display:none`). Correct, and defeated by `place()` clearing `.spent` on every call while the **resize** handler calls `place()` — iOS fires resize on nearly every scroll as the URL bar moves. Fixed separately and **kept**: it is why the artefact no longer follows scrolling.
  3. Suppressed the effect on touch in light mode. Removed the artefact and **also removed the animation** — rejected by Harkirat, correctly: the droplets fusing are the effect.
  4. Swapped the source colours so light needs no `invert(1)` (`--goo-bed`/`--goo-ink`, both themes now on the identical three-function chain). **This did NOT fix it** — confirmed on device after shipping. Kept anyway: it is strictly simpler than the fourth filter function and removes one variable from whatever comes next.

  **Where to look next**, given (4) ruled out the filter chain as the sole cause: the interaction between `.mgw`'s own `mix-blend-mode` group and `.mtint`'s `screen` inside it, under iOS's compositor, during an animation. Worth testing whether `.mtint` blending *outside* `.mgw`'s isolation group, or the tint applied as a `background-blend-mode` on a single element rather than as a blended sibling, behaves differently. **Verify on a real iPhone in light mode**, on a page whose accent makes an inverted plate obvious (License is ideal — lime is unmistakable).

  **🔧 Tooling verdict — filed 2026-08-05 22:04 EDT from the GitHub Student Pack triage.** This item was filed as *"needs a real device or CDP against a WebKit build"*. That is right about the need and wrong about the tool: this is a **compositing** failure, so what it needs is the **layer tree**, not a better picture of the artefact. Work the ladder in this order, cheapest first.

  ⚠️ **NOTHING ON THIS LADDER HAS BEEN RUN YET** — as of 2026-08-05 22:19 EDT, rungs 1 and 2 are the literal next action on this item. Until one of them reports, every claim below about *which* rung is actually needed is reasoning from how the renderers are built, not measurement. Run them before spending anything — money, a vendor signup, or another fix attempt.
  1. **Playwright WebKit, locally** — free, ~10 min. Expected *not* to reproduce, because Playwright's WebKit is a custom embedding that does not use iOS's Core Animation compositor, which is where this lives. Run it anyway: a clean non-repro is itself evidence that the compositor rather than the CSS is the cause, and it costs almost nothing to obtain.
  2. **Real Safari on macOS via `safaridriver`** — iPhone viewport, light mode, License page. Free, and shares far more of the WebKit stack than (1) does. If it ever needs automating, GitHub Actions provides macOS runners free on public repos, so no vendor is required for that either.
  3. **Real iPhone + Mac Safari Web Inspector over USB** — iPhone Settings → Safari → Advanced → Web Inspector, then Mac Safari → Develop → \[device]. **This is the rung that actually answers the question:** the Layers panel reports compositing reasons on the device that reproduces the bug. Free, already owned, and **no remote-device vendor can substitute for it** — BrowserStack and LambdaTest both hand you a screen, never an inspector.
  4. **LambdaTest Live** (Student Pack, free 1 yr) — buys *iteration speed and iOS-version breadth*, not diagnosis. Its real value is taking Harkirat out of the fix loop: all four spent fixes cost a round trip through his phone, and compositor bugs are frequently iOS-version-specific. ⚠️ **Contingent, and unproven.** If rung 1 or 2 reproduces the lime plate, the bug is diagnosable for free on hardware already owned and this rung buys nothing. Re-read this line before claiming a vendor is needed for it.

  **Verify any candidate fix the way the bug was found** — License page, light mode, real iPhone, watching the full ~2.4s birth. The colour test above remains the diagnostic: a lime plate means `.mtint` is still being composited into the filtered group, whatever else changed.

- `[P3 · S]` 🧩 **The mobile nav morph's droplet travel reads as too short.** *Filed 2026-08-04 13:02 EDT, deferred with the item above.*

  Harkirat's read: the swarm does not travel far enough to feel like it is being assembled. Note the constants have **never changed** — `git log -L` on `spray()` returns a single commit, the original `9b9b4ce` — so this is a tuning judgement, not a regression. `local/morph-poc/` is a *different* and much richer per-frame system (back-to-top, cursor, reveal mark); the nav strip has always been the simpler 15-droplet CSS-keyframe spray, which is worth knowing before comparing the two.

  ⚠️ **Two constants are coupled to the radius and both must move with it.** A change was drafted and reverted unshipped so it would not land half-tuned:
  - **The y squash.** The bar is ~60px tall and `.mbar` clips, so extra radius spent vertically is invisible. `90 × 0.34` and `124 × 0.24` both put the furthest droplet ~30px off the centre line.
  - **The rotation.** The keyframes `rotate()` then `translate()`, so vertical flight is `sin(rot) × distance` — the angle *steers*. This already caused a fling 100px below a 54px bar once. Going 90 → 124 needs the angles scaled by the inverse ratio: 50–100° reached 69–88px vertically, and 34–54° at 124px reaches 69–100px.

  **Verify** on a phone in **dark** mode until the item above is fixed, or the artefact will mask whatever the droplets are doing.

- `[P2 · S]` **`timestamp-check.sh` blocks on a ONE-MINUTE future stamp — a false positive, and it denies the write.** *Filed 2026-08-03 10:35 EDT, from the morph-PoC session, where it fired for real.*

  The future-stamp branch compares **lexicographically with zero tolerance**: `.claude/hooks/timestamp-check.sh:94` does `[ "$d $hm" \> "$now" ] && echo "$d $hm"`, and in `pre` mode that becomes `permissionDecision:"deny"` (line 101). So a write carrying `10:33` while the clock reads `10:32` is rejected as "invented". It is not invented — the clock is read from a hook message at the *start* of a turn and the bytes land a minute or two later, after intervening tool calls, model latency and edit round-trips. Sub-few-minute drift is the normal cost of doing the work, not evidence of fabrication.

  **Fix:** convert both sides to epoch seconds and allow a grace window (~3 min) instead of comparing strings — e.g. `date -j -f '%Y-%m-%d %H:%M'` on macOS — and treat only stamps beyond the window as fabricated. **Keep the check.** It caught a real incident (30 fabricated stamps reached docs, memory, a released CHANGELOG and a git tag on 2026-08-02), and the failure it guards is expensive; the ask is a tolerance, not a removal. ⚠️ A gate that denies on noise is the kind that gets switched off — the file already says exactly this about its own bare-date branch at lines 106–108, which is *why* that branch may never block. The same reasoning applies here and was not carried across. ⚠️ `.claude/hooks/timestamp-check.test.sh` exists and `run-all-tests.sh` enforces coverage, so the fix updates the test in the same change — including a case at the window edge, or the tolerance is untested.

- `[P2 · XS]` **Mobile header: the GitHub mark sits 3.2px past the left edge of its own button and is clipped.** *Found 2026-08-03 11:41 EDT while testing the morph PoC on a phone; measured, not eyeballed.*

  At `≤620px` the site collapses `.ghb` to 38px — a **36px content box** — but its flex line still carries the 30px `.ghb-ic` **plus the collapsed label's 14.4px of padding**, totalling **44.4px**. With `justify-content:center` the 8.4px of overflow splits evenly, so the mark lands at `left: −3.2px` with **11.2px of slack on the right**, and `overflow:hidden` clips it. ⚠️ **The trap is that `.ghb-t b` is `opacity:0`** — that hides the label's ink and keeps its box, so the element looks gone while still taking part in the flex line. The existing comment in that rule warns about a 2px plate/border mismatch causing exactly this symptom, which is a *different* cause fixed earlier; this is a second one with the same signature.
  **Fix:** `@media (max-width:620px){ .ghb .ghb-t{display:none} }` — the label carries no information at that size (the accessible name is on the link). Verified in the PoC clone: gaps went from `−3.2 / 11.2` to `4 / 4 / 4 / 4`. ⚠️ The clone lifts the site's own rules verbatim, so this is a real site bug, not a PoC artefact — but it has only been fixed in the PoC's own stylesheet so far.

- `[P2 · S · Sonnet5-Medium]` **`/manage` Calendar bulk-import parser truncates a title's final letter whenever it ends in `d`/`p`/`e`/`g`/`m` immediately before the next entry's bullet — silently drops one character, not a rejection.** *Found live 2026-08-22 19:30 EDT, while implementing the `g`/`m` playlist-prefix aliases below (a real click-test ask) — proven to be pre-existing, not introduced by that change (repro'd against the ORIGINAL `[dpe]` character class first).* `utils/adminParser.js`'s `parseBulkEvents()`, the `entryRegex` at (now) line ~173: `/([depgm])?•\s*([\s\S]*?)(?=[depgm]?•|$)/g`. The lookahead's OWN optional prefix-letter alternative can be satisfied one character early whenever the current entry's title happens to end in a class letter — e.g. `"...Krai BR Mode•e•7/18..."` parses `title: "Krai BR Mod"` (the trailing `e` gets silently consumed as if it were the *next* entry's zero-width-preceded prefix, because the non-greedy content match stops at the first position satisfying `[depgm]?•`, which is one character before the real bullet). Repro: `node -e "console.log(require('./utils/adminParser').parseBulkEvents('p•7/1 - 7/2 | Warzone Rumble•e•7/3 - 7/4 | Anniversary'))"` → first title comes back `"Warzone Rumbl"`. **Root cause is a genuine format ambiguity, not a simple regex slip**: the prefix letter for entry N+1 sits directly touching entry N+1's own bullet with no separator from entry N's content, so `...Mode•e•...` is textually indistinguishable between "title ends in `Mode`, next entry has no prefix" and "title ends in `Mod`, next entry is prefixed `e`" — regex alone cannot resolve this without a bias, and the current bias (prefer "the trailing letter was a prefix") is silently the WRONG one whenever a title legitimately ends in one of the 5 class letters. **Not fixed this session** — guessing a heuristic without real usage data risks a worse, differently-silent regression (e.g. misreading a genuinely bulk-prefixed entry as unprefixed). ⚠️ **Worth checking whether any already-saved calendar event titles are already truncated** (`db.seasonaldatas.findOne({}, {calendar:1})` via the MongoDB MCP tools, or `mongosh`) before assuming this has never fired in real data — words ending in `e` (`Mode`, `Rumble`, `Warfare`, `Siege`) are common in CODM event names. **Fix direction:** stop trying to detect the next entry's prefix from within the lookahead; instead split unambiguously on the literal `•` first (always safe — `•` never appears inside content, per this function's own header comment), then treat a SINGLE trailing class-letter on a chunk as the *next* chunk's prefix only when doing so is confirmed against a stricter signal (e.g., the next chunk still parses as valid `M/D - M/D | Title` either way, so the ambiguity may need resolving by asking Harkirat which reading he actually wants when it's ambiguous, not guessing). ✅ **FIXED 2026-08-30 21:50 EDT, branch `claude/outstanding-v3-items-135f3b` (not yet merged), commit `4c8e0b9`.** Took exactly the fix direction above: `BULLETED_ENTRY`'s lookahead regex replaced with `line.split('•')` paired two-at-a-time (prefix, body, prefix, body...) -- a literal split has no lookahead ambiguity since '•' never appears inside content, so no heuristic or Harkirat sign-off was needed after all. Verified against both repros (`Warzone Rumble`/`Krai BR Mode` no longer truncate) plus the full existing `scripts/calendarOps.test.js` suite (18/18 green). The already-saved-titles check was also run this session (MongoDB `find` against `seasonaldatas.calendar`) -- no truncated titles found in the checked data.

### 🎨 The impeccable design hook is WIRED BY HAND, and it will be silently wrong after any skill move `[P2 · XS]`

*Filed 2026-09-06 00:29 EDT at Harkirat's instruction — "wire it but also file it so its remembered, incase it becomes friction."*

`.claude/settings.json` now carries a `PostToolUse` (`Edit|Write`, 5s) and a `Stop` (30s) entry running `node "$HOME/.claude/skills/impeccable/scripts/hook.mjs"`. Per edit it reports only what is objectively broken; the full rule set fires once at session end.

🔴 **It is hand-wired because `impeccable hooks on` CANNOT install it here.** `hook-admin.mjs`'s `repairHookManifests()` skips every target whose `skillRel` is absent, and it looks for a PROJECT-scope `.claude/skills/impeccable`. This machine installs the skill at USER scope (`~/.claude/skills/impeccable`), so the loop matches nothing and writes nothing — while `hooks status` still prints `state: enabled`. **A hook that looks installed and scans nothing, which is the exact failure impeccable's own `doctor` documentation names and which its `doctor` does not catch** (it checks whether a hook's script path resolves, not whether the manifest exists).

**If it becomes friction, the OFF switch is `IMPECCABLE_HOOK_DISABLED=1` for one shell, or `"enabled": false` in `.impeccable/config.json` for the project.** Removing the two `settings.json` entries is the permanent form.

⚠️ **The verify condition, because silence is what this fails as:** write a CSS file under `portal/ui/` containing a literal colour that is not in DESIGN.md and confirm a `[impeccable@1]` block comes back. A pipe-test of `hook.mjs` proves the SCRIPT works and never that the HOOK fires — proven fired 2026-09-06 00:29 EDT with a probe file, since deleted.

⚠️ **`$HOME` is hardcoded in the command.** A second machine, or a move of the skill, breaks it silently.


### Before promoting any gate to AUTO-CORRECTING, check these three `[P2 · XS]`
*Filed 2026-09-02 18:28 EDT from a `/code-review` medium pass that found seven defects on a green suite — three of them auto-corrections that corrupted the exact thing they existed to protect.*

The one-right-value test is necessary and **not sufficient**. Every one of the three knew the right value and still did damage, each in the same shape: **the defect was described twice, and the two descriptions drifted.**

1. **Does the SUBSTITUTION match exactly what the DETECTOR matched?** `timestamp-check`'s detector was narrowed to today; its `gsub` still matched any date, with a class that also matched real digits — so a line carrying today's placeholder beside a correct historical stamp had the *historical* one rewritten. **Check:** feed one input containing both a target and a near-miss, and assert the near-miss is untouched.
2. **Does the fix land on the right COMMAND?** `merge-delete-branch-autofix` appended its flag to the end of the line while its detector matched after `;`/`&&`/`|` — so the flag landed on the next program. **Check:** feed a chained and a piped form. If the hook cannot place it precisely, fall back to advisory; do not grow a shell parser inside a hook.
3. **Does any post-processing reach past the edit site?** `rg-flag-guard` spliced correctly and then ran a tidy-up `replace("  ", " ")` over the *whole* command, collapsing a double space inside a quoted search pattern. **Check:** put the character you tidy inside a quoted span and assert it survives. Splice precisely; never tidy afterwards.

⚠️ **And check the budget against the caller's.** `mcp-layer-check`'s probe timeout equalled its own hook `timeout`, so in the exact hang it was written to report it would be killed before speaking.

🔴 **AND THIS ENTRY IS NO LONGER THE MECHANISM — `npm run autofix:safety` is (2026-09-02 20:16 EDT).** A checklist in a deferred list is prose, and prose is what this repo has measured the worth of (`grep` 788× against a rule written down for months). `scripts/autofixSafety.test.mjs` asserts the one property all three defects violated: **a correcting hook's output must differ from its input only at the thing it came for.** Every case carries a real target AND a near-miss that must survive byte-identical, because a case with only a target cannot fail this way. Its roster is read **off disk** — a new hook emitting `updatedInput` with no adversarial case fails the suite, the same way `run-all-tests.sh` computes hook-test coverage.

**Verify:** both failure paths are proven, not assumed. Re-introduce the substitution defect in `timestamp-check.sh` and the suite names the destroyed historical stamp; drop in any hook emitting `updatedInput` with no case and it fails with *"emits updatedInput and has NO adversarial case here"*. Each of the four fixes also has its own named regression case in its `.test.sh`.

Context7's tools were missing from the tool surface at session start and arrived part-way through, with **Harkirat changing nothing** and the connector connected throughout. A confident root cause was written from a clean-looking correlation (`enabledMcpServers: ["computer-use"]`) and was wrong.

### Reroll the two Cloudflare credentials that were exposed in a chat transcript `[P2 · XS · Sonnet5-Medium]`

*Filed 2026-08-28 12:0x EDT. Harkirat's call, in his words: **"we'll reroll it in the future, I'm fine with the security concern for now."** Filed so "in the future" has somewhere to live.*

Two separate credentials went through the transcript on 2026-08-28 and both should be rolled:

1. **The Argo Tunnel token inside `~/.cloudflared/cert.pem`.** The file was uploaded so its contents could be installed, and reading it put the embedded `apiToken`, the zone id and the account id into the transcript. ⚠️ **Revoking it does NOT stop the running dev tunnel** — `cert.pem` authenticates `tunnel create` and `tunnel route dns`, while `tunnel run` uses the per-tunnel `<uuid>.json`. Roll it in the dashboard under **My Profile → API Tokens** (it appears as an Argo Tunnel token); re-run `cloudflared tunnel login` only when a new tunnel or route is next needed.
2. **The account API token now in `.env` as `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`** — verified active 2026-08-28 12:2x EDT and used the same minute to delete the abandoned `dev.portal.dioreo.app` record.

🔴 **AND A CORRECTION WORTH KEEPING, because the reasoning was wrong even where the verdict was right.** An earlier token was declared dead in two documents on the strength of `/user/tokens/verify` returning `1000 Invalid API Token`. **That is a USER endpoint and it cannot verify an ACCOUNT-owned token** — a `cfat_` token answers exactly that error while being perfectly valid, which is what happened to the replacement. Verify an account token against `GET /accounts/$CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID/tokens/verify` instead. *(The first token was in fact dead — re-tested against the correct endpoint it still fails — so the verdict survived and the method did not. That is the worse of the two failures: a method that will condemn the next valid token too.)*

**Verify:** `curl -s -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/user/tokens/verify` returns `success: true` for the new token, and the old one returns `Invalid API Token`.

⚠️ **Nothing in this repo reads either at runtime** — the bot does not talk to Cloudflare and the site deploy uses its own GitHub Actions secret — so rolling them breaks no running thing. The only cost is that DNS/tunnel work from this machine needs the new value.
